# The names the library target publishes, one line per name: `<module path> <kind> <name>`, or
# `<module path> pub use <module>::<name>` for a name a re-export publishes that the `pub mod` chain does not
# already reach.
# What: every name another crate can reach through `src/native/lib.rs`, each with the reason it is published: an
#       item a `pub mod` chain from the root reaches, a `pub` field of a published struct, a `pub` function or
#       constant of a published type, a `#[macro_export] macro_rules!`, and each name a `pub use` publishes that
#       the chain does not already reach.
# Why:  publishing is a decision, and a split that moves a private helper into a file of its own must not take that
#       decision by writing bare `pub` where `pub(crate)` or `pub(super)` says the same thing to the caller.
#       Release 0.6.1-alpha.48 did exactly that to 31 names, 7 of them private before the split.
# How:  `source_guard/nothing_becomes_nameable_from_outside_tests.rs` reads this file and fails on a published name
#       without a line, a carried name without a line, and a line whose name is no longer published.
#       A name a release adds on purpose takes a line under a reason of its own, naming the commit that added it.
#       A name a split needs only inside the crate takes `pub(crate)`, `pub(super)` or `pub(in path)` and no line.
# How the reading is bounded: an entry starts with the module path that publishes the name, so two items of one kind
#       and name in two modules are two entries; a field, a method or a constant is keyed by the type that makes it
#       nameable; a function reads whatever count of qualifiers stands before its `fn` (`async`, `const`, `unsafe`,
#       `extern`); a published type's or `impl`'s body is read when it opens its `{` on a later line than its
#       header; a `pub use` is read whole however many lines it spans, one entry per name it publishes, with an `as`
#       rename read as the published name and a glob written `pub use <module>::*`; a re-export written without a
#       leading path is read from the module that writes it; and a gate keeps an item out only where it holds only
#       when testing, so `all(not(test), unix)` leaves the item in. The shapes and the changes this reading still
#       cannot see are named, with why, in that reader's own doc.
# A name behind a feature gate is a name of this list, because the build that turns the feature on publishes it and
#       no gate keeps it out. The three such names the tree writes today are
#       `foundation::test_only_switches fn value`, `fn value_in` and
#       `worktree::worktree_clone_conversion_fence_measurement_routes fn fence_of_the_measurement_route`.
# A member of a type a `pub use` publishes at a facade path is listed at that facade's path and under the name the
#       facade gives it, because the type's own declaration sits in a module no `pub mod` chain reaches.
# What changed on 2026-11-27: this list keys every entry by its module path and member, and covers the shapes the
#       first version of the reading could not see: a qualified function, a field, a method or an associated
#       constant of a published type, a member whose declaration opens its body on a later line, and a re-export
#       written over several lines or under a name of its own. Every name the widened reading found was already
#       published by 0.6.1-alpha.50, so this round published nothing: it narrowed the names nothing outside their
#       module reads, and wrote the one line another crate's own path needed.
# What changed on 2026-11-27, in the round after 0.6.1-alpha.51: the reading now reads the members of a type a
#       `pub use` publishes at a facade path, so the fifteen names under "Members of a type a `pub use` publishes"
#       below take their lines. No release code changed with them, so nothing here was published that a build of
#       0.6.1-alpha.51 did not already publish.
#
# Named by another crate of this package, which is what needs the reach: the fuzz sidecar
# (`package/git-policy/cli.fuzz`), the executable target (`src/native/main.rs`) and the two integration test
# targets name these by a `git_policy_cli::…` path. A `git grep 'git_policy_cli::'` over those three on
# 2026-11-27 found 75 such paths; the entries below are the ones whose module and name they spell.

# The rest of the surface 0.6.1-alpha.47 already had.
# What: every entry below was nameable at 68af165851 (0.6.1-alpha.47) with the same kind and name: an item declared
#       `pub` in a module the chain reached, or a `pub` field or method of a type published then. The release that
#       followed moved and split files without publishing any of them anew, which is why none of them needs a
#       reason of its own.
# How it was matched: by kind and name in the entry's own first folder, and for a member by the type's name and the
#       member's name. That is a reading of the old tree and not a proof that this same item held the reach then; a
#       member a later commit added to an old type, under a name another type of its folder already published,
#       would be read here as published before.
# What needs the reach: nothing outside this crate names these entries today (the `git grep` above), and nobody has
#       reviewed whether a consumer needs one, so each keeps the reach it had and its line here.
